{"type":"stream_event","event":{"type":"content_block_stop","index":1},"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","parent_tool_use_id":null,"uuid":"5ddd5975-8d30-490a-a356-893e44a36467"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TqYEMPiZPcUst9df4novWA","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Machine-readable Solution Plans (AX2)\n2\t\n3\tAX1 answers *what has this project actually composed*. This answers the next\n4\tquestion — **what are you going to do about it, and on what evidence** — in a\n5\tshape a second reader can check rather than interpret.\n6\t\n7\t```bash\n8\tnpm run crm -- solution inspect  <plan.json>          # the normalized document\n9\tnpm run crm -- solution validate <plan.json>          # the contract alone; reads no project\n10\tnpm run crm -- solution check    <plan.json>          # validate, then bind to this project's AX1 report\n11\t```\n12\t\n13\tA fourth verb answers the *next* question and is documented separately, because\n14\tconflating the two is the mistake this contract exists to prevent:\n15\t\n16\t```bash\n17\tnpm run crm -- solution verify <plan.json> --evidence <evidence.json>   # DX10\n18\t```\n19\t\n20\t```text\n21\tsolution check    is this PLAN valid, and still true of this application?\n22\tsolution verify   does this IMPLEMENTATION satisfy the plan's requirements?\n23\t```\n24\t\n25\t`check` can exit 0 on a plan nobody has built a line of — that is a fact about\n26\tthe document, not a defect. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_EVIDENCE.md` covers the rest.\n27\t\n28\tThe canonical example is\n29\t[`examples/solution-plans/lead-to-won.plan.json`](../examples/solution-plans/lead-to-won.plan.json).\n30\t\n31\t## What it is not\n32\t\n33\t**It is not a planner and not a runtime.** There is no built-in LLM, no agent\n34\torchestrator, and nothing here executes a plan, writes source, installs a\n35\tpackage, configures a provider, starts a server or deploys anything. AX2 defines\n36\ta document, checks one, and binds it to a real inspection. Who writes the plan\n37\tand who carries it out are both outside this contract.\n38\t\n39\tA plan also **cannot carry executable content**, and that holds at two levels\n40\tworth separating:\n41\t\n42\t- **by contract.** There is no command, script or effect field anywhere in the\n43\t  shape, unknown keys are refused rather than ignored, and there is no code path\n44\t  from a step to an invocation. This is the actual boundary.\n45\t- **by text filtering.** Free text is additionally matched against shell\n46\t  commands, substitutions, chaining, remote addresses and script tags\n47\t  (`PLAN_EXECUTABLE_CONTENT`). This is **defense in depth, not a security\n48\t  sandbox**: it is deliberately conservative and refuses some legitimate prose,\n49\t  and it will miss a sufficiently encoded payload. Neither outcome changes the\n50\t  guarantee, because nothing reads these fields as instructions.\n51\t\n52\t## Why it exists\n53\t\n54\t`solve-business-goal` §5 has always asked for a Solution Plan with sixteen named\n55\tparts, and every agent writes it differently: different section names, different\n56\torder, different words for \"we do not know\". A human cannot diff two of them, a\n57\tsecond agent cannot read one, and nothing can check whether the application a\n58\tplan was written against is still the application in front of you.\n59\t\n60\t## The contract\n61\t\n62\t`solutionPlanContract: 1`. The top-level shape is stable and every list is\n63\tsorted by identity:\n64\t\n65\t```json\n66\t{\n67\t  \"solutionPlanContract\": 1,\n68\t  \"revision\": 1,\n69\t  \"fingerprint\": \"…\",\n70\t  \"goal\": {}, \"metric\": {}, \"application\": {},\n71\t  \"evidence\": {}, \"decisions\": [], \"steps\": [],\n72\t  \"approvals\": [], \"acceptance\": {}, \"limitations\": [], \"problems\": []\n73\t}\n74\t```\n75\t\n76\t`revision` is the plan's own generation. `fingerprint` is a SHA-256 over the\n77\tcanonical bytes of everything except `fingerprint` itself — keys sorted at every\n78\tdepth — so two plans that say the same thing hash the same, key order and\n79\twhitespace change nothing, and a silent edit cannot hide.\n80\t\n81\t## Six decision types, and nothing else\n82\t\n83\tThe repository already has a decision hierarchy. AX2 makes each rung a value, so\n84\ta plan cannot blur \"we configured something\" into \"we wrote a package\":\n85\t\n86\t| Type | Rung | What it means |\n87\t|---|---|---|\n88\t| `configure` | 1 | a policy version, a config value or a view on an installed package |\n89\t| `extend` | 2 | an action, a policy or a record through a package's declared seam |\n90\t| `evolve` | 2 | a record gains a field or a state: bump its manifest revision (ADR-019) |\n91\t| `provider` | 3 | add or configure a provider — the gap is an integration, not a model |\n92\t| `create-package` | 4 | no installed package owns this domain |\n93\t| `propose-kernel-capability` | 5 | a generic runtime gap, raised as an ADR discussion |\n94\t\n95\t`propose-kernel-capability` is in the list so a plan can **state** it, and the\n96\tvalidator refuses it in `steps[]` — rung 5 is a proposal you write, never a step\n97\tyou take. That refusal is `PLAN_DECISION_NOT_A_STEP`, and it exists because\n98\tpatching the kernel to make a solution fit is the failure this hierarchy was\n99\twritten to prevent.\n100\t\n101\t## Evidence, in six categories and no others\n102\t\n103\t```text\n104\tobservedFacts        what the data says, with the query and its version\n105\tderivedMetrics       what was computed, and how\n106\tassumptions          what was taken as true without evidence\n107\tinferences           what follows from facts plus assumptions\n108\trecommendations      what to do, traceable to the rows above\n109\tunavailableEvidence  what could not be checked, and why\n110\t```\n111\t\n112\tThe set is closed **in both directions**: an invented category is refused, and a\n113\tmissing one is a problem. A gap that is stated is part of the deliverable; a gap\n114\tthat is omitted is a claim.\n115\t\n116\t### Citations point one way\n117\t\n118\tResolving a citation is not enough — a citation has a *direction*, and the\n119\tdirection is what stops a conclusion from being laundered into a premise:\n120\t\n121\t| A… | may cite | because |\n122\t|---|---|---|\n123\t| `observedFact` | nothing | it carries a `source`, not a derivation |\n124\t| `assumption` | nothing | it is taken as true *without* evidence, by definition |\n125\t| `unavailableEvidence` | nothing | it carries a `reason`, and is never proof |\n126\t| `derivedMetric` | observed facts, assumptions | a number comes from inputs, not from conclusions |\n127\t| `inference` | observed facts, derived metrics, assumptions | |\n128\t| `recommendation` | observed facts, derived metrics, assumptions, inferences | |\n129\t\n130\tThe table is a DAG over categories, so the citation graph is **acyclic by\n131\tconstruction**: two entries citing each other, or an entry citing itself, cannot\n132\tbe expressed. A wrong-direction edge is `PLAN_CITATION_DIRECTION`; an unknown id\n133\tis `PLAN_CITATION_UNRESOLVED`.\n134\t\n135\tEvery derived metric, inference and recommendation must cite at least one id,\n136\tand citations resolve forward or backward — order in the file does not decide\n137\twhether a plan is valid. A citation list is a **set**: repeating an id is\n138\trefused rather than deduplicated, because it does not make one source into\n139\tseveral.\n140\t\n141\t## Bound to a real application, or stale\n142\t\n143\tA plan records the AX1 report it was written against as a **derived**\n144\t`inspectionFingerprint` — a 64-character hex digest computed from the canonical\n145\tAX1 report. `validate` refuses anything that is not that shape, so a free-text\n146\tlabel can no longer sit in a slot that reads as cryptographic evidence, and\n147\t`check` recomputes it from the live report and compares.\n148\t\n149\tWhat it is: a **drift detector** over the whole composition — it catches a\n150\tpolicy version, an action that disappeared or a migration checksum that moved,\n151\tnone of which a plan's own evidence lists mention. Here they are\n184\tcodes, so a plan cannot invent a softer word for \"spend money\":\n185\t\n186\t```text\n187\tactivate_journey · auto_apply_experiment_winner · change_live_audience\n188\tchange_secrets · create_or_increase_spend · external_communication\n189\tinstall_or_configure_provider · irreversible_or_destructive · launch_ads\n190\tpublish_production · sensitive_data\n191\t```\n192\t\n193\tA `provider` step **must** carry `install_or_configure_provider`, on the step\n194\tand in the plan's approval register; the validator adds the requirement rather\n195\tthan trusting the author to remember it (`PLAN_APPROVAL_MISSING`).\n196\t\n197\tThis is a **human-actor boundary, not RBAC.** Every plan carries that limitation\n198\twhether or not its author wrote it, along with three others:\n199\t\n200\t| Code | Meaning |\n201\t|---|---|\n202\t| `PLAN_NOT_EXECUTED` | a document, not a runtime — nothing here runs, installs, deploys or modifies source |\n203\t| `APPROVAL_NOT_RBAC` | no auth, tenancy or RBAC exists, so no role is enforced anywhere |\n204\t| `EVIDENCE_NOT_VERIFIED` | an observed fact is checked for shape and citation, never for truth |\n205\t| `BINDING_IS_SOURCE_ONLY` | the bound report is AX1: source-only, and says nothing about a database, a provider's health or what is deployed |\n206\t\n207\t## Exit codes\n208\t\n209\t```text\n210\t0   the plan is valid (and, for `check`, current)\n211\t1   the plan has problems — the complete problem list is still printed\n212\t2   the plan or the project could not be read at all\n213\t```\n214\t\n215\t`inspect` reports the document without judging it, so it exits `0` even for a\n216\tplan with problems — and prints them anyway. `validate` reads **no project at\n217\tall**, so it runs in CI, in review, or against a repository that is not the one\n218\tthe plan targets.\n219\t\n220\t## Rung 3 and above must show their work\n221\t\n222\t`provider`, `create-package` and `propose-kernel-capability` all mean \"nothing\n223\tinstalled can do this\". This answers the next\nquestion — **what are you going to do about it, and on what evidence** — in a\nshape a second reader can check rather than interpret.\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- solution inspect  <plan.json>          # the normalized document\nnpm run crm -- solution validate <plan.json>          # the contract alone; reads no project\nnpm run crm -- solution check    <plan.json>          # validate, then bind to this project's AX1 report\n```\n\nA fourth verb answers the *next* question and is documented separately, because\nconflating the two is the mistake this contract exists to prevent:\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- solution verify <plan.json> --evidence <evidence.json>   # DX10\n```\n\n```text\nsolution check    is this PLAN valid, and still true of this application?\nsolution verify   does this IMPLEMENTATION satisfy the plan's requirements?\n```\n\n`check` can exit 0 on a plan nobody has built a line of — that is a fact about\nthe document, not a defect. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_EVIDENCE.md` covers the rest.\n\nThe canonical example is\n[`examples/solution-plans/lead-to-won.plan.json`](../examples/solution-plans/lead-to-won.plan.json).\n\n## What it is not\n\n**It is not a planner and not a runtime.** There is no built-in LLM, no agent\norchestrator, and nothing here executes a plan, writes source, installs a\npackage, configures a provider, starts a server or deploys anything. AX2 defines\na document, checks one, and binds it to a real inspection. Who writes the plan\nand who carries it out are both outside this contract.\n\nA plan also **cannot carry executable content**, and that holds at two levels\nworth separating:\n\n- **by contract.** There is no command, script or effect field anywhere in the\n  shape, unknown keys are refused rather than ignored, and there is no code path\n  from a step to an invocation. This is the actual boundary.\n- **by text filtering.** Free text is additionally matched against shell\n  commands, substitutions, chaining, remote addresses and script tags\n  (`PLAN_EXECUTABLE_CONTENT`). This is **defense in depth, not a security\n  sandbox**: it is deliberately conservative and refuses some legitimate prose,\n  and it will miss a sufficiently encoded payload. Neither outcome changes the\n  guarantee, because nothing reads these fields as instructions.\n\n## Why it exists\n\n`solve-business-goal` §5 has always asked for a Solution Plan with sixteen named\nparts, and every agent writes it differently: different section names, different\norder, different words for \"we do not know\". A human cannot diff two of them, a\nsecond agent cannot read one, and nothing can check whether the application a\nplan was written against is still the application in front of you.\n\n## The contract\n\n`solutionPlanContract: 1`. The top-level shape is stable and every list is\nsorted by identity:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"solutionPlanContract\": 1,\n  \"revision\": 1,\n  \"fingerprint\": \"…\",\n  \"goal\": {}, \"metric\": {}, \"application\": {},\n  \"evidence\": {}, \"decisions\": [], \"steps\": [],\n  \"approvals\": [], \"acceptance\": {}, \"limitations\": [], \"problems\": []\n}\n```\n\n`revision` is the plan's own generation. `fingerprint` is a SHA-256 over the\ncanonical bytes of everything except `fingerprint` itself — keys sorted at every\ndepth — so two plans that say the same thing hash the same, key order and\nwhitespace change nothing, and a silent edit cannot hide.\n\n## Six decision types, and nothing else\n\nThe repository already has a decision hierarchy. AX2 makes each rung a value, so\na plan cannot blur \"we configured something\" into \"we wrote a package\":\n\n| Type | Rung | What it means |\n|---|---|---|\n| `configure` | 1 | a policy version, a config value or a view on an installed package |\n| `extend` | 2 | an action, a policy or a record through a package's declared seam |\n| `evolve` | 2 | a record gains a field or a state: bump its manifest revision (ADR-019) |\n| `provider` | 3 | add or configure a provider — the gap is an integration, not a model |\n| `create-package` | 4 | no installed package owns this domain |\n| `propose-kernel-capability` | 5 | a generic runtime gap, raised as an ADR discussion |\n\n`propose-kernel-capability` is in the list so a plan can **state** it, and the\nvalidator refuses it in `steps[]` — rung 5 is a proposal you write, never a step\nyou take. That refusal is `PLAN_DECISION_NOT_A_STEP`, and it exists because\npatching the kernel to make a solution fit is the failure this hierarchy was\nwritten to prevent.\n\n## Evidence, in six categories and no others\n\n```text\nobservedFacts        what the data says, with the query and its version\nderivedMetrics       what was computed, and how\nassumptions          what was taken as true without evidence\ninferences           what follows from facts plus assumptions\nrecommendations      what to do, traceable to the rows above\nunavailableEvidence  what could not be checked, and why\n```\n\nThe set is closed **in both directions**: an invented category is refused, and a\nmissing one is a problem. A gap that is stated is part of the deliverable; a gap\nthat is omitted is a claim.\n\n### Citations point one way\n\nResolving a citation is not enough — a citation has a *direction*, and the\ndirection is what stops a conclusion from being laundered into a premise:\n\n| A… | may cite | because |\n|---|---|---|\n| `observedFact` | nothing | it carries a `source`, not a derivation |\n| `assumption` | nothing | it is taken as true *without* evidence, by definition |\n| `unavailableEvidence` | nothing | it carries a `reason`, and is never proof |\n| `derivedMetric` | observed facts, assumptions | a number comes from inputs, not from conclusions |\n| `inference` | observed facts, derived metrics, assumptions | |\n| `recommendation` | observed facts, derived metrics, assumptions, inferences | |\n\nThe table is a DAG over categories, so the citation graph is **acyclic by\nconstruction**: two entries citing each other, or an entry citing itself, cannot\nbe expressed. A wrong-direction edge is `PLAN_CITATION_DIRECTION`; an unknown id\nis `PLAN_CITATION_UNRESOLVED`.\n\nEvery derived metric, inference and recommendation must cite at least one id,\nand citations resolve forward or backward — order in the file does not decide\nwhether a plan is valid. A citation list is a **set**: repeating an id is\nrefused rather than deduplicated, because it does not make one source into\nseveral.\n\n## Bound to a real application, or stale\n\nA plan records the AX1 report it was written against as a **derived**\n`inspectionFingerprint` — a 64-character hex digest computed from the canonical\nAX1 report. `validate` refuses anything that is not that shape, so a free-text\nlabel can no longer sit in a slot that reads as cryptographic evidence, and\n`check` recomputes it from the live report and compares.\n\nWhat it is: a **drift detector** over the whole composition — it catches a\npolicy version, an action that disappeared or a migration checksum that moved,\nnone of which a plan's own evidence lists mention. What it is **not**: proof of\nauthorship, authorization or correctness. Obtaining one honestly means running\nthe tooling against a real project, which is exactly what makes it evidence of\ndrift; `accordo solution check --json` publishes the live value so an author can\nrecord it.\n\nIt covers package identities and versions, capability requires/provides and\nresolution, resources, declared action metadata, policy and provider identities\nwith their declared-definition fingerprints, record revisions and migration\nchecksums, and the problems and limitations that bound what may be planned. It\nexcludes every label, description, hint and route, plus absolute paths,\ntimestamps, config values, database state and runtime status.\n\nAlongside it, the plan lists the packages, capabilities and records it actually\ndepends on, so `check` can name *what* moved rather than only that something\ndid:\n\n| Difference | Reported as |\n|---|---|\n| the composition digest moved | `PLAN_STALE` on `inspectionFingerprint`, with both values |\n| a package is gone, or at another version | `PLAN_STALE` naming both versions |\n| a capability is gone, or stopped resolving | `PLAN_STALE` naming both statuses |\n| a record moved to another revision | `PLAN_STALE` citing ADR-019 |\n| a record declares no revision at all | `PLAN_STALE` — it is a core record, not an ADR-019 managed module, so a plan must not pin it |\n| a step needs a capability that is missing or unresolved | `CAPABILITY_NOT_AVAILABLE` |\n\nA plan whose premises have changed is not a plan, and reading one as if it were\nis how an agent confidently builds on a capability the application no longer\nhas.\n\n## Approvals: a closed vocabulary\n\n`solve-business-goal` §9 lists the sensitive boundaries in prose. Here they are\ncodes, so a plan cannot invent a softer word for \"spend money\":\n\n```text\nactivate_journey · auto_apply_experiment_winner · change_live_audience\nchange_secrets · create_or_increase_spend · external_communication\ninstall_or_configure_provider · irreversible_or_destructive · launch_ads\npublish_production · sensitive_data\n```\n\nA `provider` step **must** carry `install_or_configure_provider`, on the step\nand in the plan's approval register; the validator adds the requirement rather\nthan trusting the author to remember it (`PLAN_APPROVAL_MISSING`).\n\nThis is a **human-actor boundary, not RBAC.** Every plan carries that limitation\nwhether or not its author wrote it, along with three others:\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `PLAN_NOT_EXECUTED` | a document, not a runtime — nothing here runs, installs, deploys or modifies source |\n| `APPROVAL_NOT_RBAC` | no auth, tenancy or RBAC exists, so no role is enforced anywhere |\n| `EVIDENCE_NOT_VERIFIED` | an observed fact is checked for shape and citation, never for truth |\n| `BINDING_IS_SOURCE_ONLY` | the bound report is AX1: source-only, and says nothing about a database, a provider's health or what is deployed |\n\n## Exit codes\n\n```text\n0   the plan is valid (and, for `check`, current)\n1   the plan has problems — the complete problem list is still printed\n2   the plan or the project could not be read at all\n```\n\n`inspect` reports the document without judging it, so it exits `0` even for a\nplan with problems — and prints them anyway. `validate` reads **no project at\nall**, so it runs in CI, in review, or against a repository that is not the one\nthe plan targets.\n\n## Rung 3 and above must show their work\n\n`provider`, `create-package` and `propose-kernel-capability` all mean \"nothing\ninstalled can do this\". The hierarchy only works if that claim is *evidenced*,\nso a decision at rung 3 or higher must record every lower rung in `rungsTried`,\na reason per rung in `rejectedRungs`, and the capability `gap` no installed\npackage fills. Anything missing is `PLAN_RUNGS_NOT_INSPECTED`.\n\nRungs 1 and 2 carry no such burden: configuring or extending what already exists\nneeds no essay.\n\nA `provider` decision also adds `PROVIDER_STATUS_UNKNOWN` to the plan's\nlimitations, whatever its author wrote. A provider has five distinguishable\nstates — composed in source, configured, holding credentials, reachable,\nauthorized — and AX1 can evidence exactly the first.\n\n## Artifacts name a place, never content\n\n`acceptance.artifacts[]` says what a plan intends to produce: a closed `kind`\n(`admin-view`, `document`, `migration`, `module`, `package`, `policy`,\n`provider-config`, `test`), a repository-relative `path`, and a description.\nAn absolute path, a `..` escape, a path claimed twice, or any field carrying\nfile content is refused — an artifact holding source is the executable-content\nboundary in a different costume.\n\n## Requirement identity, derived rather than declared\n\n`decisions[]`, `steps[]` and every evidence entry already carry stable, unique,\nduplicate-refused ids. `acceptance.checks[]` is an array of **bare strings**, and\nDX10 needs to address one. It does so **without changing this contract**: a\nrequirement id is derived, not stored.\n\n```text\nstep:<stepId>        the id its author already wrote\ncheck:<32 hex>       the first 32 hex of sha256 over the acceptance-check statement\n```\n\nNothing here gains a field, a rule or a byte of fingerprint, so every plan\nalready checked in became addressable with no migration and no rewrite. The ids\nare published by `inspect`, `validate` and `check` under a top-level\n`requirements` key — **outside** the `plan` object, so no fingerprint moves.\nRewording a criterion changes its id, which is the point: evidence recorded\nagainst the old wording must not carry silently to a criterion nobody\nre-examined. Two identical statements collide and are refused\n(`PLAN_REQUIREMENT_DUPLICATE`). An **artifact is not a requirement** and neither\nis a JTBD row — ADR-031 says why.\n\n## Bounds\n\nA plan is at most 1 MiB, a text field 2 000 characters, an identifier 120, a\nlist 200 entries and a citation list 50. Every bound is a refusal, never a\ntruncation: a plan silently cut to 200 decisions reads as a complete plan.\nUnknown keys are refused at every level (`PLAN_FIELD_UNKNOWN`): silently\ndropping one means the plan claims something the reader never sees, and the\nfingerprint — computed over the normalized document — would not cover it.\n\n## Using it as an agent\n\n1. Run `app inspect --json` and read `valid`, `problems`, `limitations` in that\n   order.\n2. Write the plan, recording that report in `application`.\n3. Run `solution check`. Fix every problem before writing any code.\n4. Cite the package and capability behind each step. Report a missing capability\n   as missing.\n5. Re-run `solution check` before the review.
